- **refactor-engineer**: Refactor engineer. Use to make existing code better without changing what it does: less code, clearer structure and names, faster hot paths, each change proved behavior-preserving. - **refactor-engineer**: Behavior-preserving refactor specialist. Characterizes current behavior, slices one smell at a time, and keeps the external contract. Use for cleanup, DRY, extract, SOLID, or complexity reduction — not for features or speculative rewrites. &lt;example&gt; Context: A module is hard to change user: "This service is a 900-line mess, clean it up without changing behavior" assistant: "I'll use the refactor-engineer agent: characterization tests, then small slices." &lt;commentary&gt; Explicit no-behavior-change cleanup — this agent's contract. &lt;/commentary&gt; &lt;/example&gt; ([andrewr303/ultimate-claude-code](https://github.com/andrewr303/ultimate-claude-code/blob/HEAD/plugins/ult-engineer/agents/refactor-engineer.md))
- **refactor-engineer**: Applies narrow, reversible refactors after a plan exists. ([builtbyai/capability-library](https://github.com/builtbyai/capability-library/blob/HEAD/packages/capabilities/deepseek-router/templates/agents/refactor-engineer.md) · 2 copies)
- **refactor-engineer**: Code hygiene and architecture-enforcement engineer for Nexus Verge. Use proactively to remove dead code, collapse duplication, migrate magic numbers into rulesEngine.js, fix mechanical ADR-010 violations (hardcoded ability-ID branching → generic effect handlers), and clean up backwards-compat cruft — while proving zero behavior change via the existing test suite. Typically spawned by architect after a drift review identifies concrete cleanup targets. Never touches game-balance values, never introduces new abstractions beyond removing existing ones. ([KnowledgeRatio/nexus-verge-crpg](https://github.com/KnowledgeRatio/nexus-verge-crpg/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/refactor-engineer.md))
- **refactor-engineer**: Use when large-scale or multi-target structural refactoring is needed — complexity violations flagged by health-check, framework migrations, Boy Scout Rule debt from code-review, or an explicit modernization sprint. Builds a characterization-test safety net (via unit-tester) BEFORE refactoring, applies named Fowler operations to lower complexity and remove duplication, verifies behavior preservation (same tests green), and produces refactoring-notes.md. MUST NOT add new behavior in the same run. Invoke unit-tester first if no test coverage exists for the target. ([orieken/loom](https://github.com/orieken/loom/blob/HEAD/shared/agents/refactor-engineer.md) · 3 copies)
- **refactor-engineer**: Use when executing a refactoring plan: restructuring existing code with zero behavioral change, verified step by step. Expects the plan, file paths, and scope boundaries from the parent; does not design the refactor itself. ([rjkaes/dotfiles](https://github.com/rjkaes/dotfiles/blob/HEAD/dot-claude/agents/refactor-engineer.md))
- **refactor-equivalence-reviewer**: Compare a bounded before-and-after refactor for unintended changes in returns, errors, ordering, or side effects, returning a witness table of inputs and observations. Use when a rename, extraction, or restructuring is presented as behavior-preserving and the parent asks whether any supported input now behaves differently. Not for whether tests catch a regression; use test-gap-reviewer. ([youdede/toolkit](https://github.com/youdede/toolkit/blob/HEAD/plugins/daily-driver/agents/refactor-equivalence-reviewer.md) · 2 copies)

- **refactor-executor**: Use when a refactor spec from spec-writer is ready to execute. Executes exactly as written — no scope creep, no judgment calls. ([onlygian/G-Forge](https://github.com/onlygian/G-Forge/blob/HEAD/agents/refactor-executor.md) · ★4)
